Agreement is not the default, candor over sycophancy.\\n\\n## Subagents\\n\\nUse the [poteto role prompt](../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) for any independent worker you spawn inside a playbook step, including code-writing delegates and ad-hoc helpers. /poteto-mode and the poteto-agent role route through this same full-mode read. Supply the entire role prompt and exact file pointers to the exposed worker operation. Generic routing without that prompt and read skips the style contract. Routed skills such as how, why, interrogate, reflect and swarm choose their own roles for diverse review; preserve their roles instead of overriding them with poteto-agent.\\n\\nDefaults for every worker assignment follow the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Use independent background execution only when exposed and observed. Do not claim it from a role name or flag. Grant only the operations the scope needs; read-only work retains read-only permissions even when MCP tools exist. Use file pointers rather than inlined bulk context. Record requested and observed model and effort separately under [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md).\\n\\nCode delegates tier by difficulty. Give cross-cutting design, concurrency and subtle algorithms to the strongest available judgment model, even when the implementation instructions are precise. Trivial mechanical edits use an available fast code model. Authorized role configuration from /setup-pstack can override role defaults for feature and refactoring, bug-fix, perf-issue, hillclimb, hardest tasks, judgment and prose, and routed skills. Select only supported models and efforts from current tool documentation or observed enumeration. A missing role line keeps the portable inherit-parent default. A role line of inherit-parent or auto omits explicit model selection. Unsupported configured models or efforts require a disclosed supported fallback, or blocked if that exact choice is required. The source-host named defaults are not portable availability evidence. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified.\\n\\nWithout an exposed and authorized independent-worker operation, apply the same full-mode and leaf reads sequentially only within authorized scope. Label reduced capability. Sequential roles are not independent workers, independent review, background execution or model diversity. If the playbook requires independence, leave that gate blocked rather than silently waiving it.\\n\\nA worker forbidden to spawn owns its assigned diff directly and returns it to the lead for review separation, as Feature step 4 requires. It never waits on a nested worker or replies \\\"standing by\\\". This exception does not waive the lead's required independent review. Before accepting any result, match assignment identity, exact input revision, full current head SHA, existing artifacts and observed method evidence. Reject stale results and missing artifacts under the assignment contract. Bookkeeping labels cannot satisfy these checks.\\n\\nYou own every subagent's work. Review the diff and write your own summary, don't pass through what it said. A second opinion is the same prompt against a different model. Treat agreement as supporting evidence only after checking independence, the observed models and the underlying artifacts.\\n\\n**Fresh subagents by default.** Give new work to a fresh subagent with consolidated scope, meaning the original brief, every later directive, and the prior agent's report and branch. This holds for a fix round, a follow-up, a retry, and the next queue item. Resume, message, or queue a follow-up on an existing subagent only when the new work strictly needs state that lives in that agent and is costly to move: its local checkout, its uncommitted changes, or a process it still runs, such as a dev server, a simulator, or a babysit watcher. A stop or hold order to a running agent is not reuse. A role such as a PR owner outlives its agent. Once that agent returns, a fresh agent takes the role's next round. Interrupt-chained resumes silently drop directives, so fire a fresh subagent with consolidated scope rather than trusting a \\\"done\\\" summary.\\n\\n## Throughput checkpoint\\n\\nFor nontrivial multi-step work, write these four todo items before fan-out, as required by Feature step 3. Keep an inapplicable dimension with n/a and a reason. Rewrite the checkpoint at phase boundaries and return it with the evidence.\\n\\n- Blocking first steps. Gates run before fan-out.\\n- Independent workstreams. Disjoint files, services or layers parallelize. Shared writes serialize.\\n- Shared mutable state. Default to splitting the target using the separate-before-serializing-shared-state principle leaf. Serialize only for real invariants.\\n- Smallest safe decomposition. If one worker is best, name why.\\n\\nCode-coupled work goes to one owner with the checkpoint inline. That owner fans out after the blocking phase when authorized and supported. Parent-level fan-out is for independent artifacts. Assignment records and bookkeeping do not prove workers ran. Keep decision trails in an explicit owned local output path per show-me-your-work and its log helper contract. Missing helper setup or persistence stays visible; do not invent saved logs or cross-session continuity.\\n\\n## Integrated helper and delivery bindings\\n\\nRead the selected playbook's complete adapted helper and prompt contracts before dependent execution. The 23 bundled playbook routes below and the Bugbot rubric are local instructions. Historical task deferrals in those files are not proof of absence or permission to skip a present contract. Check the actual path. An absent required contract still blocks its dependent step.\\n\\n- Decision trails use [show-me-your-work](../show-me-your-work/SKILL.md), its [template](../show-me-your-work/references/decision-log-template.tsv) and [log helper](../show-me-your-work/scripts/log.sh).\\n\\n  Invoke the helper through authorized execution with exactly `<logfile> <phase> <decision> <why> <evidence> <result>`.\\n\\n  The nonempty logfile is an explicit owned local path.\\n\\n  Tabs, newlines and carriage returns are rejected before writes, and formula-leading cells are quoted.\\n\\n  Read back the trail and retain the skill's scoped …768 tokens truncated…gets across re-arms and retain an observed stop or deadline binding for a hard wall-clock limit.\\n\\n  The helper deadline is checked between polling work.\\n\\n  Do not add a second sleep loop or treat GitHub output as Origin evidence.\\n- Cleanup uses the [audit wrapper](scripts/worktree-audit.sh) and [implementation](scripts/worktree-audit.py) through its playbook with an explicit local `--repo` and verified `--base`, plus authorized `--history-map` and `--pr-state` exports when available. The helper returns logical bytes and evidence buckets, performs no fetch or deletion, and leaves unknown facts as holds. A bucket never grants deletion permission. Recheck actual usage and ignored files before separately authorized removal.\\n\\nThe [bootstrap](scripts/bootstrap.ts), [manifest](scripts/package.json) and [lock](scripts/bun.lock) require exact commander 14.0.0, bun-types 1.3.14 and typescript 7.0.2 metadata before dependency-backed CLI execution. Bootstrap fails setup-required and never installs. Run helpers with Bun runtime auto-install disabled. Missing dependencies remain blocked. The isolated tested snapshot only supports that snapshot, not this caller, the current checkout or either host. [Helper setup](../../docs/testing/helper-setup.md) states the exact-copy validation boundary and retains historical setup evidence. It does not authorize installation or transfer a historical pass to this caller. Any future setup needs fresh authorization for an exact writable copy, registry, disabled lifecycle scripts and task-owned cache. Never install in an operative invocation or bypass bootstrap.\\n\\nDelivery reads [Opening a PR](playbooks/opening-a-pr.md) in full and retains its review, companion, commit, push, draft or ready authorization and actual-result gates. A local draft is not a created PR. An exposed built-in PR tool owns operations it covers, and a failed tool does not authorize a CLI bypass. Opening alone never starts Babysit. A read-only question stays in Investigation, with no PR; its specific exception takes precedence over the generic delivery route. Prototype and planning handoffs grant no implementation or external permission. Missing create-skill, deslop, matching controls or independent review remains an explicit blocked gate even when a reduced local artifact is useful.\\n\\n## Writing the reply\\n\\nWrite the reply clean as you draft it. A cleanup pass after drafting does not remove these patterns.\\n\\n- **Short declarative sentences.** One thought per sentence, ended with a period.\\n- **No long-dash character anywhere.** Write a file-list bullet as a sentence (\\\"`main.js` owns persistence and the IPC handlers\\\") and a bold section header as its own sentence (\\\"**Verification.** End to end via CDP\\\").\\n- **A colon as a mid-sentence connector is also out** (unslop rule 14). A colon before a list is fine.\\n- **Terse is not an excuse to drop content.** Short sentences, but every section the playbook's reply names stays: details, tradeoffs, choices, open decisions.\\n- **Frame impact for the consumer and the maintainer.** Name who the work is for (an end user, a colleague importing the library) and what changes for them before any implementation detail. Then what the next engineer who owns this code inherits. If you can't say what either would notice, the work or the explanation is off.\\n- **Never fabricate a link, citation, or transcript reference.** Link only artifacts you produced or read this session.\\n- **Every claim carries its evidence or its label in the same sentence.** Measured, inferred, or guess. A prediction or an unseen cause is a guess. Never hand the human a check you could run.\\n\\nEvery playbook ends with a reply written this way. Link to the actual PR you read or created when authorized. Never claim a draft is a created PR. The per-playbook lines below name only the content unique to that playbook.\\n\\n## Comments\\n\\nComments follow the same rule as the reply. Write them clean as you go. Keep a comment only for a non-obvious *why* the code can't show. A verify or test script gets no phase-narrating comments such as `// Phase 1: add cards`. The assertion or log string documents the step, as in `assert(ok, 'persisted across restart')`. This applies to every file you produce, including the delegate's diff.\\n\\n## Playbooks\\n\\nOpen an available todo list, or an authorized local Markdown checklist if no todo operation exists. Record that reduced tool binding. Its first items are the matched playbook's steps, copied in verbatim, before any task-specific todos. A step you choose not to do stays in the list with a one-line `skip: <reason>`. Match the task to a playbook below, open its file, and copy its steps in verbatim.\\n\\nA large or cross-cutting effort (a migration across many call sites, an ambitious multi-part change), or work the user steps away from to trust later, routes to the **figure-it-out** skill even when a narrower playbook like Feature fits. Use **figure-it-out** whenever no bundled playbook fits. It designs a bespoke, rigorous playbook for the task. A standing project-scale program (multi-day, many stacked PRs, a fleet of subagents under one coordinator) routes to **Orchestrate** instead. figure-it-out designs one bespoke run, orchestrate runs the program.\\n\\n- **Investigation.** Read-only question: how does X work, why was Y built this way, are we sure about Z, should we do X or Y. `playbooks/investigation.md`.\\n- **Bug fix.** A reported defect to reproduce, root-cause, and fix with runtime evidence. `playbooks/bug-fix.md`.\\n- **Perf issue.** A measured slowness to trace and improve against a baseline. `playbooks/perf-issue.md`.\\n- **Hillclimb.** Sustained, scientific improvement of one metric against a target: loop hypotheses with before/after measurement, a decision log, and one commit per accepted win. Distinct from Perf issue, which is a one-off fix. `playbooks/hillclimb.md`.\\n- **Runtime forensics.** Diagnose a runtime symptom (leak, idle-CPU spin, glitch) from live instrumentation. The deliverable is a diagnosis, not a fix. `playbooks/runtime-forensics.md`.\\n- **Trace forensics.** Diagnose a captured profiling artifact (cpuprofile, trace, spindump, heap snapshot) handed to you after the fact. The deliverable is a diagnosis, not a fix. `playbooks/trace-forensics.md`.\\n- **Feature.** New or changed behavior, built from a named data shape. `playbooks/feature.md`.\\n- **Refactoring.** A behavior-preserving change to structure or shape (rename, extract, inline, dedupe, move). `playbooks/refactoring.md`.\\n- **Prototype.** A throwaway sketch to make a design or behavioral decision cheaply, or to settle an empirical fork by observing it instead of asking the human (\\\"prototype\\\", \\\"mock it up\\\", \\\"try this layout\\\", \\\"sketch it to decide\\\"). `playbooks/prototype.md`.\\n- **Visual parity.** Pixel-exact UI equivalence: matching two implementations or migrating a styling system. `playbooks/visual-parity.md`.\\n- **Authoring or modifying a skill.** Writing or editing a SKILL.md. `playbooks/authoring-a-skill.md`.\\n- **Eval.** Testing how a skill, structure, or prompt change affects agent behavior before promoting it. `playbooks/eval.md`.\\n- **Babysit.** Driving a PR or a stack to merge-ready: conflicts, review threads, CI. `playbooks/babysit.md`.\\n- **Shipping.** The half after Babysit. Independently verifying a green stack, then landing the contiguous verified run bottom-up through `gh` by default or Origin when its CLI is available. `playbooks/shipping.md`.\\n- **Autonomous run.** A long task to drive to completion without stopping (\\\"run until done\\\", \\\"/loop until X\\\"). `playbooks/autonomous-run.md`.\\n- **Orchestrate.** A standing project handed to one coordinator chat: multi-day, many stacked PRs, dozens to hundreds of subagents, minimal human turns (\\\"run this whole project\\\", \\\"own this migration until it lands\\\"). Distinct from Autonomous run, which drives one task to a predicate. Work one agent could finish inside the session's budget routes there, not here, however program-shaped the phrasing sounds. `playbooks/orchestrate.md`.\\n- **Autopilot-full.** A queue of independent PRs run to merged with full autonomy. One owner per PR carries build through merge, and the root swarm-verifies each PR before its owner merges (\\\"autopilot this queue\\\", \\\"full autopilot\\\", one-owner-per-PR programs). `playbooks/autopilot-full.md`.\\n- **Autopilot-stack.** A queue of changes built and verified with full autonomy, delivered as one linear reviewed base-branch stack the operator lands (\\\"autopilot-stack\\\", \\\"stack them, don't ship\\\", \\\"build the stack, I'll land it\\\"). `playbooks/autopilot-stack.md`.\\n- **Session pickup.** Resuming or taking over a prior agent's in-flight work from a transcript, cloud-agent URL, or pushed branch. `playbooks/session-pickup.md`.\\n- **Pause safely.** Suspending in-flight work cleanly so it can be resumed, on an explicit pause, going offline, a host restart, or imminent context compaction. The complement to Session pickup. Full steps: `playbooks/pause-safely.md`.\\n- **Multi-phase or multi-PR plan.** Work that spans phases or stacked PRs. `playbooks/multi-phase-plan.md`.\\n- **Worktree and simulator cleanup.** Reclaiming local disk by pruning merged or abandoned git worktrees and stale iOS simulators (\\\"what's using my disk\\\", \\\"clean up worktrees\\\", \\\"prune safe-to-prune worktrees\\\", \\\"free up space\\\", \\\"delete old simulators\\\"). `playbooks/worktree-cleanup.md`.\\n- **Opening a PR.** Invoked at the end of every other playbook. `playbooks/opening-a-pr.md`.\\n### Authoring or modifying a skill\\n\\n**You own the skill's voice.**\\n\\n1. Use the **create-skill** authoring skill when the session exposes it. This package does not ship that built-in dependency. Discover an available authoring workflow through [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), read its instructions and inspect its actual validator before using it. A supported host workflow may provide a capability-specific alternative, but is not the missing create-skill implementation. If neither workflow is available, report authoring blocked. With authorized local writes, offer a provisional draft and manual structural checklist as reduced capability. Do not claim the required authoring workflow ran.\\n2. Validate the skill: frontmatter has `name` and `description`, referenced files exist, cross-skill links resolve. Report the validator used, its actual results and any checks it does not cover. If execution is unavailable, mark validation unverified and report the checklist findings separately. Structural validation does not verify trigger performance; leave unknown trigger performance unverified.\\n3. Test cases if structural. Skip if subjective. Read-only tests use fixtures. Report what ran and what remains unverified.\\n4. Run [Opening a PR](opening-a-pr.md) within the active [workflow ownership](../../../references/workflow-ownership.md), actual capabilities and user authorization. Until that dependency is available, retain the validated local artifact or provisional draft and report PR delivery deferred. Commits and external writes require their own authorization. Do not claim a PR exists without an observed result.\\n\\nWhen in doubt, delete. Keep only prose that changes a decision. Tell it to do the thing and skip the reason. Explain only when the rule is confusing without one. Match tone to scope. Point at structural sources such as types, READMEs and config per the [encode-lessons-in-structure principle](../../principle-encode-lessons-in-structure/SKILL.md). Delegate to other skills by path. Don't restate. Propose a new skill for a workflow you keep hitting that is not captured.\\n\\n**Reply:** summary of the skill, key design decisions, validation notes. Include the authoring workflow actually used, missing dependencies, skipped subjective tests, unknown trigger performance and PR delivery status.\\n### Shipping\\n\\n**You own what lands. Verify each PR independently, land only the verified run from the root, then keep your hands off the queue.**\\n\\nThis is the half after [babysit](babysit.md). It owns requests to land or ship. Apply [workflow ownership](../../../references/workflow-ownership.md), [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md) and the [assignment and action contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md).\\n\\nRecord the active forge, repository, exact trunk tip, frozen bottom-to-top PR list and each verifier assignment before action. Check explicit user authorization for merge or merge-when-ready. Posting verdicts, publishing branches and retargeting each require their own authorized scope and available binding. Verification alone authorizes none of them. Prepare local artifacts when writes are denied. After interruption, inspect the prior action identity and destination before replay; an unknown outcome blocks replay.\\n\\nUse the caller's watch budget or at most 10 snapshot attempts and 600 seconds for this invocation. Carry the remaining budget across re-arms and merges. Stop on user stop, exhausted budget, denied access or unavailable operation, preserving the last observed frontier and unknowns. Use an exposed authorized loop or wake binding when available. Without one, a bounded foreground watch is reduced capability and registers no background continuation. A helper deadline is checked between reads and sleeps; it is not a hard wall-clock bound. Use an observed stop or execution-deadline binding for that guarantee, or disclose the limitation.\\n\\n1. **Resolve the forge, then verify every PR independently.** GitHub CLI (`gh`) is the default. If `command -v origin` succeeds and Origin can resolve the repository, use `origin pr ...` for PR view, watch, edit, and merge operations. Otherwise stay on `gh` and record the fallback. Never require Graphite (`gt`). One subagent per PR, not batched, each an independently spawned worker through an observed host binding, each exercising the real surface with the matching exposed and authorized control skill against exact parent versus head revisions. The upstream examples `control-ui` and `control-cli` are external skills, not bundled bindings. Missing control capability leaves real-surface verification blocked. Use inherited model roles unless current supported selection and actual results are observed. Each returns `PASS`, `PASS+NOTES` or `FAIL`, with verifier identity, exact revisions and method artifacts, and posts that verdict on its own PR when comment authorization exists. Otherwise retain the verdict locally and report that posting is blocked. A missing worker permits local sequential recommendations as reduced capability, never an independent verdict or a landable run. Safe means a verdict from an agent that did not write the code. CI green is not a verdict, and an approving bot review is not a verdict.\\n2. **Land only the contiguous verified run rooted at the bottom.** Walk up from the lowest unmerged PR and stop at the first one without a passing verdict, where both `PASS` and `PASS+NOTES` pass. A verified PR sitting above an unverified one is not landable. Report the ceiling as a PR number and say what breaks the chain.\\n3. **Re-check that each verdict still describes the patch.** Record the verdict head SHA, base SHA, and stable `git patch-id` of that PR's base-to-head diff. A rebase or base retarget rewrites SHAs and can silently invalidate a verdict without touching a check. Before landing a PR, read and record its current exact head SHA and base SHA, resolve the base-to-head diff at those revisions, and compare the recorded patch-id with its current base-to-head patch-id. Re-read the head and base immediately before the authorized merge or arm action. If either moved since the freshness check, stop and repeat that check. When the two patches differ only in tests, docs, or lint config, build what each lane ran. Build it twice at the verdict SHA and once at the current head. A difference is noise if the two builds at the verdict SHA also show it, or if it is an embedded commit SHA. Judge each difference, not each file, and report each kind of noise with its files. If only noise differs, that lane's result stays valid, and checks and a review of the change run fresh. Do not reuse a lane result from a dev server or from anything else with no build output. Rerun that lane. Re-verify anything else when the patch changed. When it did not, keep the code verdict but re-run mergeability and CI at the current head. Never use matching commit messages or a green check from an older SHA as a substitute.\\n4. **Prepare only the bottom PR.** Fetch current trunk. Rebase the lowest verified branch onto the exact trunk tip when needed, push it, and retarget only that PR to trunk through an exposed built-in PR tool when present, as required by [opening a PR](opening-a-pr.md). Without that tool use `origin pr edit <pr> --base <trunk>` or `gh pr edit <pr> --base <trunk>`. Re-run step 3 after the push. Do not retarget, arm, or merge descendants yet.\\n5. **Land one PR at a time.** If the bottom PR is mergeable now, squash it with `origin pr merge <pr> --squash` or `gh pr merge <pr> --squash`. If requirements are still running and the user asked for merge-when-ready, arm only that PR with `origin pr merge <pr> --squash --auto` or `gh pr merge <pr> --squash --auto`. Origin's `--auto` is Origin merge-when-ready. GitHub's `--auto` is GitHub auto-merge. Wait for that PR to merge before preparing the next one.\\n6. **Do not read GitHub `autoMergeRequest` as stack readiness.** At most it says GitHub auto-merge was requested for one GitHub PR. It does not prove Origin merge-when-ready is armed, that a descendant is queued, that a patch verdict is current, or that the contiguous stack is safe. Confirm the active forge's state for the current bottom PR, and say that the state is unknown if the active forge cannot report it.\\n7. **Recompute after every merge.** Fetch trunk, confirm the merged SHA is present, drop the merged PR from the frozen bottom-to-top list, and inspect the new bottom PR's base, head, checks, and patch-id. A host may retarget a child automatically, but do not assume it did. Repeat steps 3 through 6 for that one PR. Independent work stays outside this chain and ships on its own.\\n8. **Watch the current frontier until it merges or fails. Do not mutate the queue around it.** With Origin, use `origin pr view <pr> --checks --comments` and `origin pr checks <pr> --watch`, then re-read the PR until it reports merged or blocked. With GitHub, use the read-only [watcher](../scripts/watch-pr/watch-pr) from the package root as `bun --no-install skills/poteto-mode/scripts/watch-pr/watch-pr --owner <owner> --repo <repo> --queued-stack --stack-prs <bottom> --head <bottom=full-40-character-head> --max-polls <remaining-count> --timeout <remaining-seconds>` only as an event wake and poll `gh pr view <pr> --json state,mergedAt,mergeStateStatus,statusCheckRollup,autoMergeRequest` after each wake, ignoring `READY` until `mergedAt` is non-null or `state` is `MERGED`. Only then run step 7. Hard-fail only when `state` is `CLOSED` with no `mergedAt`, a required check concludes `FAILURE` or `CANCELLED` and blocks merge after auto-merge is no longer pending, or `mergeStateStatus` is `UNSTABLE` or `DIRTY` with no auto-merge pending. `BLOCKED` while checks are pending or auto-merge is armed is not failure. Do not use Babysit's queued `WAITING`/`merge-queue` stop condition here. Hold the watch through the observed authorized loop or wake binding within the declared budget. Do not add a second sleep loop. The watcher emits NDJSON schemaVersion 1 events. Inspect kind, terminal and reason, never exit 0 alone. Queued mode emits non-terminal `WAITING` with `reason.kind` equal to `merge-queue`, not `READY`. Terminal helper blockers or budget exhaustion require inspection of the active forge, not an invented merged or hard-failed state. The [bootstrap](../scripts/bootstrap.ts) checks pinned dependencies and fails setup-required; never install or bypass it. [Helper setup](../../../docs/testing/helper-setup.md) is separately authorized. For Origin, use a finite bound and an observed stop binding for check watching, or bounded view snapshots when an unbounded watch cannot be stopped. Report each merge and the new ceiling. If the queue stalls, diagnose before mutating.\\n9. **Stop at the ceiling.** When the verified run is merged, report what landed, what the next unverified PR is, and what verifying it would take. Extending the run is a new pass through step 1.\\n\\n**Reply:** the verified run and its ceiling, each PR's verdict and who produced it, what you armed and how you confirmed it, what landed, and what the next gap needs.\\n### Orchestrate\\n\\nRead [poteto-mode](../SKILL.md), [capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md), [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md), [workflow ownership](../../../references/workflow-ownership.md), and the [poteto role prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) in full. This playbook responds to \\\"run this whole project\\\" and \\\"own this migration until it lands\\\". Aliases do not register host commands. Read applied principles and routed skills before dependent work. Use [arena](../../arena/SKILL.md), [figure-it-out](../../figure-it-out/SKILL.md), [show-me-your-work](../../show-me-your-work/SKILL.md), [separate shared state](../../principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state/SKILL.md), and [encode lessons](../../principle-encode-lessons-in-structure/SKILL.md) where the steps call for them. Apply [unslop](../../unslop/SKILL.md) to briefs and reports.\\n\\nA program needs observed independent worker operations, isolated ownership, execution, persistence and result inspection. Without workers, authorized sequential work is reduced capability and cannot satisfy a required independent review or standing program. Without execution or writable persistence, block helper-dependent work and offer a scoped read-only handoff. Missing wake or remote placement means foreground operation only. Recurring wakes need explicit scheduling authorization. No unattended continuity is implied. Commits, pushes, PR creation, merges, messages, deployments, deletion and setup each retain their applicable permission gates. Gate defaults never grant permission by silence.\\n\\nThe companion wake mechanism is a host capability, not a bundled loop skill. Pending caller paths are `playbooks/autonomous-run.md` and `playbooks/babysit.md`. Stop dependent routes that need those instructions until they exist. The embedded small-task collapse in Frame remains usable without the Autonomous run document. Never install missing companions implicitly.\\n\\n**You own the program, never the code. Author briefs, drain the queue, keep the frontier green, decide.** For a whole project handed to one standing coordinator chat: multi-day, many stacked PRs, dozens to hundreds of subagents, the human checking in twice a day instead of every five minutes. One task driven to a predicate is Autonomous run. One ambitious run needing a bespoke workflow is figure-it-out. Route here when the work outlives any single agent. Work one agent could finish inside the session's budget is not a program.\\n\\nCeremony must scale with the program. On cheap near-identical units, collapse it as each section directs.\\n\\nThe following rules govern the program.\\n\\n- Completions are queue events, not interrupts.\\n- Every spawn and every resume carries the standing orders verbatim.\\n- The brief is the product. A vague brief fails quietly, because a worker cannot ask you a question.\\n\\n#### Roles and placement\\n\\n- **Coordinator (this chat).** Local. Frames, authors briefs, drains the inbox, owns the human report, makes judgment calls. It never authors or edits code. Conflicted merges, restacks, and code changes are always tasks. Mechanically landing a verified unit (fast-forward or clean cherry-pick of a worker's commit, then push) is bookkeeping the coordinator may do itself on repos where local git is cheap. Queueing finished work behind an idle stacker is how a deadline harvests nothing. Bind spawn, follow-up, wait, result inspection and stop to exposed authorized operations. State reads and writes go through the bookkeeping helper at drain points. Most compact outputs are bounded summaries; inbox drain returns all pointers, status returns three lines, and `--json` returns complete rows. The CLI never spawns, waits, or wakes anything.\\n- **Sub-coordinator.** Always local, durable, one per track, and only when the program exceeds what one coordinator's drains can manage. A track the coordinator can drain itself needs no middle layer. Each nested layer re-pays a full orientation preamble, and a blocking sub-coordinator hides its children while the parent idles. Owns its track's units and boards, authors its workers' briefs, spawns its own workers and verifiers only when nesting and isolation are exposed and observed. Depth 3 is the desired ceiling, not a portable runtime guarantee. Rolls up aggregates at wave boundaries. Never forwards raw child reports. Cap in-flight children at what one drain can process, roughly ten, as a rolling window. Never as blocking batches, which cost the slowest child of every batch.\\n- **Worker / verifier.** Prefer remote workers when exposed and authorized. Local-only verification, this run's accessible transcripts, simulators, local IDE state and local auth require a suitable local binding. Discover control-ui and control-cli companions rather than assuming them. Remote workers cannot be assumed to read the local store, so inline required context or use paths confirmed readable by that worker. Prefer fewer, broader workers. One writer per worktree or branch (principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state). Prefer a verifier on a different observed model family. Inherit the parent selection unless explicit supported selection is observed. Unknown actual models leave diversity unverified; an unavailable required family blocks that gate.\\n\\nDepth stays at coordinator, track, worker. Author the track decomposition per project (build, landing, and verification are common cuts, not a required shape). Hard-coded swarm trees were tried and parked as too rigid.\\n\\n#### Store layout\\n\\nChoose an explicitly authorized task-owned writable `orchestrate/<project-slug>/` outside the package tree. Every file has exactly one writer. Owners publish facts, readers aggregate at read time. Use `bun --no-install <package-root>/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/orch/orch.ts --store <owned-store>`, written below as `orch`. `ORCH_STORE` is the alternative explicit binding. Plain TSV and JSON stay readable without the CLI.\\n\\n- `preferences.md` is the standing-orders register: numbered lines, one constraint each (model policy, stack shape and count, verification bar, forbidden paths, escalation policy). Paste it verbatim into every spawn and every resume. Directives decay across resumes, and each dropped one costs a human turn. When you catch yourself restating an instruction, append the line before you act (principle-encode-lessons-in-structure).\\n- `overview.md` is the durable PR and issue DB. Append. Never rewrite wholesale per event.\\n- `units.tsv` has one row per unit: id, track, state, branch, PR, head SHA, brief path. Update rows in place.\\n- `frontier.json` is the computed merge frontier, per Stack safety.\\n- `ledger.tsv` is the verification ledger, per Verification.\\n- `inbox/` holds completion pointers. `gates.md` parks human gates (question, options, default on no answer).\\n- `decisions.tsv` is the trail via the show-me-your-work skill.\\n- `status.md` is derived from `units.tsv` and `ledger.tsv` at each drain, never hand-maintained. Regenerate it from the tables instead of narrating events into it.\\n\\n\\n#### Helper contract\\n\\nRead [orch CLI](../scripts/orch/orch.ts), [store](../scripts/orch/store.ts), [tests](../scripts/orch/orch.test.ts), [bootstrap](../scripts/bootstrap.ts), [manifest](../scripts/package.json) and [lock](../scripts/bun.lock). The CLI requires the exact pinned dependencies in a separately authorized writable copy. The operative package has no installed dependencies; the tested helper snapshot is evidence for that snapshot only. No automatic install occurs. Read docs/testing/helper-setup.md for the exact-copy validation boundary and retained historical setup evidence. It grants no installation approval and transfers no historical pass to this caller. Any future setup needs fresh explicit authorization for the exact writable copy, registry, disabled lifecycle scripts and task-owned cache under Herd. Missing dependencies block execution.\\n\\nUse these actual commands through the explicit `orch` binding:\\n\\n- `init`; `standing add <line>`; `standing show --json`. Compact standing output truncates after four rows, so use JSON or read preferences.md to obtain every standing order.\\n- `unit add <id> --track <track> --brief <path>`; `unit set <id> --state <state> --branch <branch> --pr <number> --sha <full-head-sha>`; `unit get <id>`; `unit list --json`.\\n- `ledger record <pr> <full-head-sha> <verdict> --evidence <path> --verifier <identity>`; `ledger check <pr> <full-head-sha>`; `ledger summary`. A missing exact key returns NOT-VERIFIED and exit 2. Usage or runtime failure returns exit 1; success returns 0.\\n- `inbox push <agent> <unit> <status> --report <path>`; `inbox drain --peek --json`; `inbox drain --json`; `inbox count`. Peek before drain, persist that receipt to an owned artifact and read it back. Capture the complete actual drain output to another owned receipt and verify readback before classifying pointers. Drain removes the queued pointers; arrivals between peek and drain can change the actual batch. If the receipt is lost or interruption makes consumption uncertain, reconcile actual artifacts before retrying or updating rows.\\n- `gate park <id> --question <question> --options <options> --default <answer>`; `gate list --json`; `gate resolve <id> --answer <answer>`. Resolve permission gates only from actual authorization.\\n- `frontier set --repo <stack-owner-clone>`, optionally `--prs <bottom-to-top-numbers>`; `frontier show --json`; `status`. Frontier discovery runs local git and gt inspection against that clone. Missing gt metadata, malformed output or pin mismatch is an error, never guessed frontier state.\\n\\nThe helper does not spawn, wait, wake, enforce worker ownership, validate full revision syntax for unit or ledger input, inspect evidence paths, enforce terminal states or verify permissions. Keep immutable assignments and append-only attempts separately under the assignment contract. Match identity, input revision, full current head SHA, artifacts and observed method before accepting results. Ledger counts include historical revisions; filter against the current unit and frontier heads before counting verified completions. A done unit string, ledger label or open-gate count proves no worker completion or authorization. Coordinator-owned bookkeeping may record worker facts; give each worker separate result and receipt paths. Atomic rename is not proof of crash durability or safe adversarial concurrent access.\\n\\n#### The brief\\n\\nYour prompts to agents are your only product, and a sloppy brief compounds into slop across the whole tree. Every spawn carries all of it. A field you cannot fill is a unit you have not scoped yet.\\n\\n```\\nGOAL         one sentence, the outcome, executable by a stranger with no chat access\\nSCOPE        paths this unit may write; paths it may not; its exclusive worktree or branch\\nCONTEXT      pointers to files and PRs; upstream reports pasted in full when this unit\\n             depends on them, because workers cannot see siblings\\nACCEPTANCE   checkable criteria, one per line\\nVERIFY       exact commands or the control-skill path, plus known gotchas\\nTIMEBOX      rough cap on runtime; on expiry, return partial findings and stop rather than run on\\nFORBIDDEN    no gt, no rebase, no force-push, no fixes outside scope, plus unit-specific bans\\nREPORT       status, branch, head SHA, PRs, verdict, what you actually ran, deviations,\\n             suggested follow-ups\\nSTANDING     <preferences.md pasted verbatim>\\n```\\n\\nSize the brief to the unit. A one-command unit gets the template collapsed to a paragraph that still names goal, scope, the verify command, and the report shape. A 4KB scaffold around a two-line edit costs more to write and obey than the edit. A verified readable local standing-orders path may supply the exact text. Paste it verbatim for remote spawns and every resume. Never replace it with a summary.\\n\\nA sub-coordinator brief adds its track boundary and unit list, its spawn budget with supported remote placement and the local exception list, the drain protocol, and the rollup format (per child: name, status, PR, head SHA, verdict, one line, plus track status and frontier delta).\\n\\nA dependency is a context relay, not just ordering. Undeclared upstream context makes the worker guess. Missing fields are a refuse-to-spawn condition. Audit one sampled worker brief per sub-coordinator per wave, concurrently with the wave it samples, never as a gate in front of it. A failing brief stops that track and fixes the sub-coordinator's instructions, not just the worker, because brief quality decays late in a run. Never resume-chain a brief. Respawn fresh with consolidated scope.\\n\\n#### Steps\\n\\n1. **Frame.** State the done predicate as something countable (\\\"all 126 units merged, each verified at each current full head SHA with `unit-test-verified` or better\\\"). Quantify scope: units, rough effort, expected stacks, and the wall-clock budget. If one agent could finish inside that budget, stop here and run Autonomous run instead. Collapsing must not depend on another document being present. It means do the work directly in this session, plain workers where they help, verification inline, landing as you go, and none of the store, register, or pilot machinery below. Schedule landing against the budget. By roughly 70% of it, stop spawning and land what is verified. Name the tracks per project. A contested decomposition or one-way door goes through the arena skill before the pilot. Present the framing once. Reversible prep proceeds without waiting.\\n2. **Initialize the runtime.** Check the helper prerequisites, then run `orch init`. Initialization creates bookkeeping files, not dependencies or workers. Open the trail via the show-me-your-work skill, write the standing orders before any spawn, and seed `frontier.json` from existing PRs with `orch frontier set --repo <repo-dir>`.\\n3. **Pilot.** Push one unit through the whole path: brief, worker, verification, stack entry, ledger row, merge. The pilot exists to falsify the brief template, the verify recipe, and the unit size while that costs one agent instead of fifty. Fix the contract from pilot evidence before any fan-out. Scale the pilot to the unit. On programs of near-identical cheap units, the first unit is the pilot, run as a normal unit with its verify command inline, and fan-out starts the moment it lands. The dedicated pilot pipeline (separate verifier agent, audit gate) is for expensive or novel unit shapes, not for clone-units where a serialized pilot has nothing to falsify.\\n4. **Scale.** Spawn a rolling window of workers up to the in-flight cap, refilling as children finish. Blocking batches pay the slowest child of every batch. Spawn track sub-coordinators only past the one-drain threshold in Roles. Recompute ready work after each drain. Relay upstream reports into downstream briefs. Keep sibling communication upward only. The sampled brief audit runs alongside the wave it samples and stops the next refill on failure, not the current one.\\n5. **Drain.** Run the queue discipline below at every drain point.\\n6. **Land.** Landing is continuous, never a terminal phase. Integration starts with the first verified unit and runs alongside the remaining waves. On heavy repos the stacker is a standing role from wave one, integrating as units verify. On repos where local git is cheap, the coordinator lands verified units itself per Roles. Keep the frontier green before upper-stack work. Stack safety governs. Advance `frontier.json` only on merge or reported new head SHAs.\\n7. **Close.** Drain the final inbox, reconcile every spawned agent to a terminal row (done, abandoned, zombie-reconciled), confirm the predicate on the real artifact, confirm every landed PR has a verdict for its current head SHA, audit the trail per show-me-your-work including its cross-model review, encode recurring corrections into `preferences.md` or the brief template. Leave the store intact. It is the postmortem.\\n\\n#### Queue and drain\\n\\n- On a completion notification, run `orch inbox push <agent> <unit> <status> [--report PATH]` and return to what you were doing. Never deep-review inline. A completion that needs review becomes a verifier unit. Never review a diff inside a drain.\\n- Drain in batches at four points: the end of a critical section, a track rollup, a frontier watcher wake (arm it through an exposed authorized wake binding, with a long heartbeat fallback when explicitly authorized), and before a human report. Begin each batch with the peek-and-receipt protocol above, then `orch inbox drain --json`. Preserve completion notifications that arrive during drain for the next batch. A concurrent helper writer can fail on the store lock; keep that pointer externally and enqueue it at the next safe boundary instead of treating it as saved.\\n- Critical sections you finish first: authoring a brief, a stack operation, a conflict decision, writing a gate, updating ledger or frontier.\\n- Each drain classifies every pointer (landed, needs-verify, failed, zombie, noise), writes the resulting rows through `orch unit add`, `orch unit set`, and `orch ledger record`, runs `orch status`, then spawns the next wave in one message.\\n- Account for every spawned child at its track's rollup: arrived, respawned, or its scope explicitly absorbed. Silently redoing a missing child's work hides both the wasted spend and the coverage gap its result existed to close.\\n- A drain turn ends with the three lines from `orch status`: counts against the states, what changed, gates open. Detail lives in `status.md`. The full reply contract applies at checkpoints and close.\\n\\n#### Stack safety\\n\\n- The frontier is a computed object, never narrative. Recompute `frontier.json` from `gt` after every merge and stack mutation because GitHub base refs drift mid-restack while gt tracking is authoritative: ordered PR list, branch names, head SHAs, a generation number, the lowest unmerged PR. Resolve it where gt knows the stack, normally the stacker's clone. A checkout whose gt metadata never saw the submits reports no PRs and the command errors rather than guessing.\\n- Exactly one stacker per stack may run `gt`, serialized within its stack. Record the holder in the standing orders. Prefer remote restacks when supported and authorized. Missing remote placement remains a limit; never silently substitute local heavy work.\\n- Workers never rebase and never run `gt`. Babysitters follow `playbooks/babysit.md`, one per stack, scoped to one immutable frontier generation. They report conflicts to the stacker rather than restacking.\\n- PR closes and retargets go through the stacker only. Closing a base PR orphans every chain above it. Merges and stack surgery are units with briefs like any other.\\n- One retro watcher follows merged PRs for reverts, post-merge CI breaks, and orphaned follow-ups.\\n\\n#### Verification\\n\\nScale verification to the unit. When VERIFY is a single cheap command, the worker runs it and reports the output, and the coordinator spot-checks receipts. A dedicated verifier agent (on a different model family than the worker) is for units whose verification is expensive, judgment-laden, or high-blast-radius. A verifier agent whose entire product would be rerunning one command is ceremony, not verification.\\n\\nWrite ledger rows with `orch ledger record`. Check the current PR and head SHA with `orch ledger check`. `ledger.tsv`, one row per verdict, keyed by PR number plus head SHA: `live-ui-verified | unit-test-verified | type-check-only | verifier-blocked | verifier-failed`. CI green is an input to a verdict, not a verdict. Behavioral work needs better than `type-check-only`. `verifier-blocked` is not a pass. Respawn when the environment heals. `verifier-failed` gets a fix unit, not a re-verify. A worker may self-report. The coordinator inspects the method evidence and lets a verifier override it on the same key. The helper replaces a row on that key regardless of role; callers must enforce precedence and preserve attempt history separately. A new head SHA voids the row, so re-verify after restack. The ledger answers \\\"was this verified\\\", not memory and not the transcript.\\n\\nA unit is not done until its output is externalized the moment it lands, never batched to the end of the run. An authorized worker pushes its branch, the designated ledger owner records the inspected verifier result, and receipts land in the store. Without push permission, externalization is limited to the authorized durable artifact and landing remains pending. Work that exists only on one VM when that VM dies was never done.\\n\\n#### Liveness and failure\\n\\n- Never resume an agent to check on it. A follow-up can restart an idle worker; inspect its actual state before use. Probe read-only: the ledger, `units.tsv`, `gh`, pushed branches, the worker's observed status through the exposed inspection binding. Transcript mtime is not liveness.\\n- A silent death gets a synthetic postmortem row in the inbox (unit, failure mode, last evidence, options). Replan on evidence as it arrives. Never wait for full quiescence.\\n- Retry by mode: cap-hit or oom, respawn with smaller scope. Network-drop, retry as-is. Tool-error, retry on a different model. Unknown, retry once. Two retries, then abandon the unit and replan around it. Missing required SHA or method evidence uses the stricter assignment limit of one inspected retry. A changed scope or input revision gets a new assignment ID linked to its predecessor. Never retry denied permissions or unknown external outcomes into success.\\n- A zombie that returns hours late reconciles against the current frontier and ledger before anything is accepted. Salvage unique findings through a fresh unit, never a blind merge.\\n- When continued spawning would produce garbage tree-wide (bad upstream output, broken acceptance, dead infra), write a stop line at the top of the standing orders, let in-flight work finish, fix the cause, clear it.\\n- Bound your own infra retries the same way you bound a child's. After a few consecutive tool aborts, stop retrying. Write a terminal handoff to durable state (what is done, where it lives, the exact command to resume) and end the run.\\n- After a host restart, inspect worker state. Do not infer that local agents died or remote work survived. Re-read the standing orders and `units.tsv`, recompute the frontier, reattach surviving remote work by inspected PR and branch plus immutable assignment identity rather than stale agent id, respawn one sub-coordinator per track from its stored brief plus current state, drain, resume. A dead holder does not authorize takeover. `orch` rejects a stale lock until ownership review. Use `--force` only after explicit reviewed takeover permission; it can also steal a live lock.\\n\\n#### Escalation\\n\\nReaches the human, batched into the status page rather than per item: irreversible actions (force-push to shared branches, deploys, deletions, closing someone else's PR), genuine product or preference calls no experiment settles, a standing order that contradicts observed reality, a program-level dead end that survived a replan. Park each as a `gates.md` entry before asking, and route work around it.\\n\\nNever reaches the human: frontier nudges, restack mechanics, retries, CI flake triage, review-thread triage, format fixes, scope the brief already forbids (refuse and continue), and \\\"should I keep going\\\". Act and log within existing authorization. Park any missing permission instead of assuming it.\\n\\nMid-run discoveries fix only what blocks the frontier. Everything else parks in follow-ups. At this fan-out a small scope leak multiplies into PRs nobody asked for.\\n\\n**Reply:** at checkpoints and close: the predicate and the count against it from `units.tsv` and `ledger.tsv`, tracks and what each landed, the frontier (PR list plus SHAs), verdicts summary, what was abandoned and why, gates awaiting the human (the only asks), the store path, and the trail path. Numbers from the tables, not narrative. Include PR links.\\nimport { readFileSync } from \\\"node:fs\\\";\\nimport { join } from \\\"node:path\\\";\\n\\nconst pins = { commander: \\\"14.0.0\\\", \\\"bun-types\\\": \\\"1.3.14\\\", typescript: \\\"7.0.2\\\" } as const;\\n\\n// Keep the caller API, but setup is always an explicit operator action.\\nexport function ensureDependenciesInstalled(): void {\\n  try {\\n    const manifest = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(import.meta.dir, \\\"package.json\\\"), \\\"utf8\\\"));\\n    // Bun's text lockfile is JSON with trailing commas.\\n    const lock = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(import.meta.dir, \\\"bun.lock\\\"), \\\"utf8\\\").replace(/,\\\\s*([}\\\\]])/g, \\\"$1\\\"));\\n    for (const [name, version] of Object.entries(pins)) {\\n      const requested = manifest.dependencies?.[name] ?? manifest.devDependencies?.[name];\\n      if (requested !== version) throw new Error(`${name} must be pinned to ${version}`);\\n      if (lock.packages?.[name]?.[0] !== `${name}@${version}`) throw new Error(`${name} lock resolution must be ${version}`);\\n      const installed = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(import.meta.dir, \\\"node_modules\\\", name, \\\"package.json\\\"), \\\"utf8\\\"));\\n      if (installed.name !== name || installed.version !== version) throw new Error(`${name} installed version must be ${version}`);\\n    }\\n  } catch (error) {\\n    throw new Error(\\\"Helper setup required: prepare a writable dependency copy using docs/testing/helper-setup.md. \\\" + String(error));\\n  }\\n}\\n# Control-adapter contract\\n\\nBenny does not know how to start or drive every app. The user must configure one control skill or adapter that implements this contract for the target app.\\n\\nSet its skill name in `control.skill_name`.\\n\\nSet the completed user-facing feature map path in `control.feature_map_path`. Copy and fill [`feature-map.example.md`](./feature-map.example.md) outside `automations/benny/` instead of editing the copied example.\\n\\nIf the skill, feature map, or a required capability is absent, ambiguous, or incomplete, repro and fix work must fail closed.\\n\\n## Required capabilities\\n\\n### Bring up\\n\\nStart the requested app revision in the requested test environment.\\n\\nInput:\\n\\n- Repository and revision\\n- Build or start mode\\n- Workspace, account, fixture, and feature-state requirements\\n- Artifact directory\\n- Completed feature-map path\\n\\nReturn:\\n\\n- Session identifier\\n- How the adapter confirmed the correct app and environment\\n- Stable app markers\\n- Running process or target details needed by later calls\\n- Any missing capability\\n\\nThe adapter must distinguish the target app from a similar window, shell, or production instance.\\n\\n### Drive UI\\n\\nPerform real user actions:\\n\\n- Click\\n- Type\\n- Press keys\\n- Scroll\\n- Drag\\n- Resize\\n- Navigate through app controls\\n\\nPrefer roles, labels, and stable selectors. Use coordinates only after a fresh screenshot.\\n\\nReturn each action and the observed state change.\\n\\nDo not set internal state, call hidden app methods, write directly to storage, or inject DOM changes to create the symptom.\\n\\n### Drive mapped features and states\\n\\nRead the relevant feature-map section before driving the app.\\n\\nThe adapter must expose ways to:\\n\\n- Navigate every mapped feature through the user-visible path.\\n- Invoke the adapter action names listed for that feature.\\n- Interact with default, hover, focus-visible, active, disabled, loading, empty, error, selected, open, expanded, and feature-specific states when they apply.\\n- Arrange a state through safe fixture data, permissions, flags, service responses, or supported test controls.\\n- Reset the feature for a second independent repro attempt.\\n- Capture the screenshot, video, and read-only cross-check named by the feature map.\\n\\nUse roles, accessible names, ARIA relationships, stable component markers, and purpose-named data attributes. Never use generated CSS or StyleX classes, dynamic hashes, child indexes, or brittle DOM position.\\n\\nArranging a precondition is not permission to inject the reported symptom. The repro itself must still come from real user interaction.\\n\\n### Inspect state\\n\\nRead state to confirm what the UI shows.\\n\\nExamples:\\n\\n- Accessibility tree\\n- DOM or view hierarchy\\n- Process state\\n- Local logs\\n- Network request status\\n- App-exposed debug state\\n\\nInspection is read-only. If a query changes state, it belongs in `drive UI` and must represent a real user action.\\n\\n### Screenshot\\n\\nCapture the current app state to a requested path.\\n\\nReturn:\\n\\n- File path\\n- Capture time\\n- App marker or window title\\n- Short description of what should be visible\\n\\nThe screenshot must show enough app chrome to prove that the correct app is under test.\\n\\n### Recording\\n\\nStart and stop a screen recording around the full repro path.\\n\\nReturn:\\n\\n- File path\\n- Start and stop times\\n- Captured window or region\\n- Whether audio or sensitive overlays were omitted\\n\\nThe recording must show the discriminating final state, not only setup or a loading screen.\\n\\n### Cleanup\\n\\nStop processes and sessions created by the adapter.\\n\\nRemove disposable:\\n\\n- Browser or app profiles\\n- Temporary workspaces\\n- Test accounts or fixtures when the adapter created them\\n- Debug ports and tunnels\\n- Captures past their retention window\\n\\nReturn what was stopped, removed, retained, or left for a person.\\n\\nCleanup must not delete user work.\\n\\n## Adapter behavior\\n\\nThe adapter must:\\n\\n- Report capabilities before the repro starts.\\n- Report which feature-map sections it can drive and which are blocked.\\n- Use the same environment inputs for baseline and patched builds.\\n- Surface startup failures as failures.\\n- Bound retries.\\n- Keep secrets out of logs and artifacts.\\n- Keep captures outside the repository.\\n- Support a fresh or reset state between the two repro attempts.\\n- Avoid production changes unless the user explicitly configured a safe test action.\\n\\n## Environment translation\\n\\nBefore declaring an environment block, restate the defect without platform-specific nouns and ask whether the same behavior can be tested safely in the available environment.\\n\\nExamples:\\n\\n- A named browser may mean any external browser.\\n- A named key may mean the configured shortcut.\\n- A named remote host may mean a delayed or disconnected remote target.\\n\\nUse a translated attempt only when it tests the same underlying behavior. Label it as translated evidence. Do not call it an exact repro when the missing environment is part of the defect.\\n\\nHardware prompts, operating-system permission dialogs, device-only APIs, and unavailable account states may be real blocks.\\n\\n## Setup check\\n\\nBefore enabling the repro automation, first review supplied or inert evidence for this adapter check. Actually starting apps, driving disposable states, capturing media, or cleaning fixtures requires separately authorized test scope. With that scope, run one harmless adapter check:\\n\\n1. Bring up the app.\\n2. Confirm the stable app marker.\\n3. Load one completed feature-map section.\\n4. Navigate to that feature through its user path.\\n5. Exercise one disposable state through mapped adapter actions.\\n6. Inspect the resulting state.\\n7. Capture a screenshot.\\n8. Record a short clip.\\n9. Clean up.\\n\\nEnable repro work only when all nine steps succeed and no source-channel Slack post is involved.\\n\\nMissing or denied capabilities block repro. Fixture review is structural only and cannot satisfy the nine live adapter steps.\\n\"}}\n"

      "text": "{\"args\":{\"cmd\":\"cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/reflect/SKILL.md'\",\"max_output_tokens\":10000},\"result\":{\"chunk_id\":\"a9d831\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000006708,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":1456,\"output\":\"---\\nname: reflect\\ndescription: Spawn three parallel review subagents over the active transcript, surface learnings, and route each to a concrete edit on an existing skill. Use when the user says reflect.\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Reflect\\n\\nMine the current conversation for durable learnings, then route them into skill edits.\\n\\n## When to invoke\\n\\nInvoke when the user says \\\"reflect\\\" or \\\"/reflect\\\". Skip when the conversation is trivial, off-topic, or already covered by an existing skill the parent followed correctly. One-offs are not learnings.\\n\\n## Process\\n\\n### 1. Locate the active transcript\\n\\nThe parent discovers the active session through authorized exposed history or file tools. Read [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md), and [workflow ownership](../../references/workflow-ownership.md). Record observed read, history, spawn, result inspection, execution and write bindings as applicable. Preserve the active controller.\\n\\nUse only the transcript location supplied for this active workspace or an exposed active-chat reference. Verify its opening user prompt before use. Do not search unrelated workspace or user chat directories. If no active transcript resolves, write a tight session digest, mark its omissions, and pass that instead. If neither authorized history nor a usable digest exists, stop with blocked evidence-backed reflection.\\n\\n### 2. Spawn three reviewers in parallel\\n\\nWhen authorized independent workers are exposed, assign three reviewers in parallel under the assignment contract. Give each an immutable revision, role, separate output ownership and the complete corresponding template. Reviewers may read transcript-referenced context through available authorized tools but may not write code, edit skills, commit or send messages.\\n\\n| Lens | Selection role | Prompt template |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| Judgment | reflect judgment, divergent, synthesizer | [judgment reviewer](references/judgment-reviewer.md) |\\n| Tooling | reflect tooling | [tooling reviewer](references/tooling-reviewer.md) |\\n| Divergent | reflect judgment, divergent, synthesizer | [divergent reviewer](references/divergent-reviewer.md) |\\n\\nUse the model-role contract for each selection role. Honor an explicitly supplied authorized role preference only when its model and effort are currently supported; otherwise retain inherit-parent and disclose the limit. Record requested and observed values separately. A rejected choice requires disclosed supported fallback or a block when the exact choice is required. Different role prompts do not establish model diversity.\\n\\nPass each template in full, substituting the transcript path or digest where marked. Inspect every worker result and its artifacts; a completion notification is insufficient. If independent workers are unavailable, perform the same three lens passes sequentially as reduced capability, followed by synthesis. Do not claim independent review.\\n\\n### 3. Synthesize\\n\\nAssign an independent synthesizer under the same capability, model-role and assignment contracts when available. Give it the [synthesizer template](references/synthesizer.md) in full with each reviewer's full output inlined where marked. Its spot-verification uses only authorized transcript-referenced context reads. Inspect its result. In the reduced sequential branch, apply the same synthesis prompt after all three lens passes. The synthesizer returns a structured Accepted / Rejected / Backlog list.\\n\\n### 4. Structural enforcement check\\n\\nSanity-check the synthesizer's Accepted list. For any item that would be enforced more reliably by a lint rule, script, metadata flag, or runtime check, move it from Accepted to Backlog. See the [encode lessons in structure principle](../principle-encode-lessons-in-structure/SKILL.md).\\n\\n### 5. Apply\\n\\nBefore applying any Accepted edit, present the synthesizer's full Accepted/Rejected/Backlog output to the user and wait for explicit approval. The user picks which subset to apply and may redirect routings. Skill changes affect future agents that load the changed skill. Do not auto-apply.\\n\\nFile Backlog items to the team devex tracker only when the user has explicitly authorized that tracker write and the exposed tool permits it. Otherwise provide an unfiled Backlog draft and state the limit. Backlog filing never authorizes Accepted edits.\\n\\nFor each approved Accepted item, follow the Routing field exactly:\\n\\n- Trivial existing-skill edit (a one-line bullet, a tightened sentence, a stale fact corrected): parent does directly.\\n- Substantive existing-skill edit (a new section, a new pattern table, more than ~10 lines): hand to an available authorized skill-authoring workflow and run its draft / test / iterate loop.\\n- `tune description: <skill path>` (the skill exists but didn't trigger when it should have): hand to that skill-authoring workflow and run its description-optimization loop.\\n- `new skill via create-skill: <kebab-name>`: hand creation to that skill-authoring workflow. Do not invent the shape ad hoc.\\n\\nIf a required authoring workflow is unavailable, keep the approved proposal as a draft and report application blocked. Do not claim its draft / test / iterate or description-optimization loop ran.\\n\\nIf your environment ships an authorized SKILL.md validator, run it on every touched skill before declaring done. Skip this step if it doesn't.\\n\\n### 6. Summarize for the user\\n\\nShort list, no preamble:\\n\\n- Edits applied: `<skill path>`. What changed, one line each.\\n- New skills created: `<skill path>`. One line each (rare).\\n- Backlog filed to the devex tracker: `<issue title>` (`<tags>`). One line each.\\n- Dropped: one line per rejected finding + reason from the synthesizer.\\n\"}}\n"
